Not done: the glyph is initials, not a per-route icon glyph set — two routes can share initials ("Sipariş istisnaları"/"Sipariş yönetimi" both "Sİ"), which the tooltip and accessible name disambiguate but the eye does not; a real icon font is a design asset this issue does not include. The DPI claim rests on storing DIPs, not on a rendered test at 125%/200% (the test host has one scale). The splitter drag is persisted on `DragCompleted` only — a window-resize that squeezes the column is not written back, so the stored width is the last *chosen* one. The collapsed state is per data directory, so two profiles keep separate choices. No animation on toggle.

## #813 — breadcrumb semantics across workspaces (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-813-workspace-breadcrumb-semantics off codex/issue-812-sidebar-collapse-persistence

New issue. DESIGN: #810 gave the dashboard a context-carrying trail, but every other way into a product, an order or a source — the global search, the saved views, the sidebar — still went through `Navigate(key)`, landed on a screen with nothing selected, and gave Back nothing to restore. And a crumb that named a product did not select it on landing (#810's own open item).

`WorkspaceLinks.cs` (new, pure): `monobridge://<kind>/<id>[?store=<key>]` is the one way to open an entity from anywhere, for the kinds this build has entities for — `product`→products, `order`→orders (`marketplace|shop|id`), `source`→xml. `Parse` treats the link as untrusted: the **route is always taken from the kind table, never from the link**; another scheme, an unknown kind (including `settings`, and `report`), an empty/oversized id, extra path segments, raw whitespace or control characters, `..` in any form, or any query key other than `store` yields no target at all rather than a partial one, and a title lookup that throws is not a reason to open a half-built target. `Format`/`Parse` round-trip composite ids and store keys through escaping. `FromSearchHit` turns a search hit into a link only when its type and route agree and it names something a workspace can select — an order hit without its store stays a plain screen jump.

`MainWindow.Navigation.cs`: `AllowedStoreKeys()` reads the **enabled connections on disk** (a failure to read allows nothing but the all-stores scope, so an unreadable store table cannot widen what a link opens); `OpenWorkspaceLink(target)` is the single entry point — the same `DrillThroughStack.Open` refusal as the dashboard, then `SelectRoute`, then `RevealEntity`; `RevealEntity` selects the product in the grid (re-searching by SKU when it is off the current page), the source in the list (selecting the instance the list holds, after the refresh that replaces them) and the order through the panel's own reveal; `Back_Click` now re-selects what the crumb names; `DrillEntityAlive` learned `source`. `OrdersPanel.cs`: `Create(..., exposeReveal)` hands back a reveal that reloads, clears the filters that would hide the order, selects it (selection opens the detail) and scrolls to it. `MainWindow.UX.cs`: a search hit that names an entity opens it on the trail; any other hit is the screen jump it always was. `DashboardPanel.cs`: quick cards are filtered through the window's real route table.

**Finding, recorded not hidden:** the shell registers no `"reports"` route. `ScreenParityAudit.RequiredRoutes` lists it (so the parity line logs BLOCKED at every startup), the dashboard's "Raporlar" quick card navigated to it (a silent no-op — `SelectRoute` returns when the key is missing), and `ReportAnalytics`/`ProductOrderReport` are only used inline in the product card. The issue's fourth workspace therefore does not exist as a workspace. This branch does **not** invent one: there is no `report` link kind (a test pins that), and the dead quick card is dropped by the route check instead of pretending. Registering a real reports workspace is its own issue.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/WorkspaceLinkTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): each kind parsing to its own route with its entity; format/parse round-trips of composite order ids, reserved characters and store keys; nineteen hostile or malformed links producing nothing; the route coming from the table for every kind; search-hit mapping (product, source, composite order; bare order, audit and type/route disagreement refused); and the report link refused. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/WorkspaceLinkWindowTests.cs` (new, 3): through a real `MainWindow` seeded with two products, a source and one enabled + one disabled connection — a product link selecting the product and a source link selecting the source, then Back re-selecting the product; a crumb whose product was deleted degrading to the product screen with the name gone from the trail; and links naming `allegro|shop-b`, `ALLEGRO|shop-a` and `etsy|shop-a` all refused while `allegro|shop-a` opens.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=WorkspaceLinks.cs (new), MainWindow.Navigation.cs, MainWindow.UX.cs, OrdersPanel.cs, DashboardPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/WorkspaceLinkTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/WorkspaceLinkWindowTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=401 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 392); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 758d1aed2b7551e46c8a86aeaf5d54e79103e3371235445fde5b60bc9ff47ba6.
Not done: no reports workspace, as above — the `"reports"` route is missing from the shell and this branch records it rather than registering a placeholder. The search index's order hits carry only the order id in `TargetId` (marketplace and shop are in the display text), so an order found through global search still opens the orders screen without selecting the row; `FromSearchHit` is ready for a composite id the moment the index writes one, and that is an index change, not a navigation one. The order reveal is not covered by a window test (the panel's grid is private to its closure; the reveal path is exercised through `RevealEntity` only for product and source). Links are opened from code paths, not from a registered URI scheme — there is no OS-level `monobridge://` handler. Revealing a product that is off the current page rewrites the product search box to its SKU, which is visible and reversible but is a side effect the operator did not type.

## #814 — global toast queue policy (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-814-toast-queue-policy off codex/issue-813-workspace-breadcrumb-semantics

New issue. DESIGN: the app had no toast at all — `Log(text)` wrote the status line, the history list, the log file and an audit row, with no severity, no lifetime, no dedupe and no way to dismiss; an error and a "kaydedildi" looked the same and both vanished under the next line.

`NotificationPolicy.cs` (new, pure): `NotificationSeverity`, `NotificationRequest`, `Toast` and `NotificationQueue`, driven by an injected clock. A severity owns its lifetime (`DurationFor`: success 4 s < info 6 s < warning 10 s; **error has none — it waits for a person**). The same text at the same severity inside a 30-second window is **one toast with a count**, refreshed rather than re-stacked; the same text at another severity is different news. At most **3** are on screen; the rest wait in arrival order and are promoted as slots free up, a promoted toast's clock starting when it is shown; the waiting line has a ceiling of 50 that drops the oldest *non-error* and counts the loss — an error is never the one dropped. `Expire(now)`, `Dismiss(id)`, `DismissTop()` (Escape closes what appeared last) and `DismissAll()` (the pending ones too). Every text goes through `AuditStore.Sanitize` (#791: bearer/basic auth, query and key=value secrets, e-mails, profile paths), then loses newlines and is capped at 240 characters; an empty text becomes a generic sentence rather than an empty box.

`MainWindow.xaml`: a `ToastHost` overlay (top-right over the module tabs, tab-cycle, live region). `MainWindow.Notifications.cs` (new partial): `Log(text, severity)` does everything `Log(text)` does **and** raises a toast; `Notify(request)` raises one with an optional action that navigates through the ordinary route (only when the route exists); `RenderToasts` draws glyph + word + text + `×N` + a focusable close button per toast, sets an automation name that includes the count, and marks errors assertive; a one-second `DispatcherTimer` expires and promotes, and stops itself when the host is empty. `Log(text)` is unchanged for its fifty callers (Info stays on the status line — a toast for every informational line would be the wall the issue is against). The **twelve error-path call sites** (`Log(Safe(e))` and the search-index one) now publish at `Error`, so real failures reach the toast host without touching the global exception handlers (which still do not set `Handled`). `MainWindow.UX.cs`: Escape dismisses the newest toast **only while the keyboard is inside the host**, so the product-inspect drawer keeps its own Escape.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NotificationPolicyTests.cs` (new, 7, red first): a burst of twenty showing three and queueing seventeen in order with nothing dropped; a triple duplicate collapsing to one toast with count 3 and a refreshed clock, a different severity and an out-of-window repeat both being fresh; per-severity clocks with the error surviving an hour and going only on dismiss; expiry promoting the waiting line with fresh clocks; keyboard dismiss removing the newest and letting the next one in, dismiss-all clearing the pending line; secret, e-mail, profile path and newlines never reaching the text, a 5000-character text capped with an ellipsis, a blank text replaced; and the pending overflow dropping the oldest non-error, counting it and never the error. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NotificationWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): the real shell — an error published through `Log(text, Error)` appearing in the host with a sanitized automation name, its repeat showing "2 kez" instead of a second toast, Escape with focus inside the host closing the newest (a success) and leaving the error, and the error's close button emptying and collapsing the host.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=NotificationPolicy.cs (new), MainWindow.Notifications.cs (new), MainWindow.xaml, MainWindow.UX.cs, MainWindow.Filters.cs, MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NotificationPolicyTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NotificationWindowTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=409 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 401); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 cf4728cca4965185d44ce8d724b8d22f432c703f5a0b0864d7db217ea1736779.
Not done: the panels (`OrdersPanel`, `SyncPanel`, the channel panels…) still report through their own status `TextBlock`s and `MessageBox.Show` (twenty-odd sites) — they do not have a handle to the window's queue; routing them through `Notify` needs the panels to receive a publisher, which is #816's error-banner work and the natural moment to do it. No toast has an action yet in production (`Notify` supports one; no caller passes one). The dedupe key is exact text, so two errors that differ only by an id stack separately, which is arguably right and arguably noisy. No animation, no hover-pause of the clock, and no "N more waiting" indicator on the host — the pending count exists on the queue but is not drawn.

## #815 — status tooltip content standard (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-815-status-tooltip-standard off codex/issue-814-toast-queue-policy

New issue. DESIGN: status tooltips were ad hoc — the product row (#794) printed badge + reason + stock, the dashboard's connection rows and the API-health rows had none, and nothing said when a state last changed, where it came from, or what to do about it. There was also no rule against a tooltip carrying a raw response body.

`StatusTooltip.cs` (new, pure): `StatusTooltipContent(Status, Reason, LastChangeUtc, Source, NextAction, Detail)` and `Compose(content, nowUtc)` — one labelled line per field, in that order, **each hidden when empty** (no "—" placeholder lines; an empty status still says "bilinmiyor"). The reason is a sentence: newlines collapse, it is capped at 240 characters with an ellipsis, and a reason that `LooksLikeRawPayload` (a JSON or XML body, a stack trace) is replaced by "Ham yanıt gizlendi; ayrıntı için Tanılama ekranına bakın." rather than shown. `Relative` gives "az önce / N dk önce / N sa önce / N gün önce" (a future timestamp says "gelecekte" rather than a negative number) next to the absolute local time in the operator's culture. `NextActionFor(stateKey)` is the shared next step for pending/error/stale/**unsupported**/warning/disabled, so a surface with nothing more specific to say still points somewhere — and "unsupported" says so instead of suggesting a retry that cannot work. The assembled text passes `AuditStore.Sanitize` last (bearer/basic auth, query and key=value secrets, e-mails, profile paths), then is capped at 400.

Owners wired to the template: `ProductRowState.Tooltip` now composes through it (badge as status, reason, `UpdatedUtc` as last change, the source record as source, stock as detail, the shared next step) — the existing test surface for #794 keeps passing; `MainWindow.Filters.cs` sets `ToolTipService.ShowsToolTipOnKeyboardFocus` and `AutomationProperties.HelpText` on every product row so the tooltip opens from the keyboard and is read by assistive tech; `DashboardPanel.cs` projects a `Tooltip` per connection row (status, last error, last test, `channel / shop`, a retest step unless connected) bound through a `DataGridRow` style with the same two properties; `ApiHealthPanel.cs` does the same in `LoadingRow` with a next step per health state (`LIVE_API_BLOCKED` reads as unsupported, `RATE_LIMITED` says wait, `AUTH_ERROR` says renew).

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/StatusTooltipTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): pending/error/stale/unsupported each composing with their own distinct next step; empty fields hidden and an empty status still a line; a 20-line reason becoming one capped sentence; five raw payloads (JSON object, JSON array, XML declaration, XML fragment, stack trace) hidden while a sentence that merely mentions a token is kept, and a bearer token, e-mail, profile path and query secret never surviving whichever field they were in; relative + absolute last-change in the current culture with the future guard; and the product row tooltip starting with the badge, carrying reason, stock and a next step. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/StatusTooltipWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): a real `MainWindow` with a disabled product — the realized row's tooltip is the template, `ShowsToolTipOnKeyboardFocus` is on, `HelpText` equals the tooltip, and the row takes keyboard focus.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=StatusTooltip.cs (new), ProductRowState.cs, MainWindow.Filters.cs, DashboardPanel.cs, ApiHealthPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/StatusTooltipTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/StatusTooltipWindowTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=416 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 409); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 77a144bda912a7049e86464a88fa975e3a1415abd99925e0167b7d1b0c1ccd18.
Not done: the sync-job grid and the channel panels' listing grids still have no row tooltip — they are the next owners to route through `Compose`, each a `LoadingRow` handler like the API-health one; the product row's "source" is the source **id** ("Kaynak kaydı <id>"), because the row classifier has no store handle to resolve the name and #800 already shows the name on the card; `LooksLikeRawPayload` is a heuristic (leading brace/bracket with a colon, XML, stack frames, two or more JSON keys) and a prose reason that happens to open with "[" would be hidden — the diagnostics pointer keeps that harmless; the keyboard-focus claim rests on the WPF property being set and the row taking focus, not on a rendered popup, which the test host cannot observe.

## #816 — recoverable error banner actions (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-816-recoverable-error-banner off codex/issue-815-status-tooltip-standard

New issue. DESIGN: a workspace failure was a modal `MessageBox` (the panels' shared `Button`/`AsyncButton` helpers) or a status-line sentence (the dashboard); neither said whether trying again could help, none offered a way to retry, reach the source of the problem or open diagnostics, and a repeat produced another box.

`ErrorBanner.cs` (new): `Classify(error)` is one rule set for every workspace — a busy/locked database (`SqliteBusyDiagnostics`), a network failure, a timeout, a cancelled request or an I/O error is **retryable**; a validation failure (`InvalidOperationException`/`ArgumentException`) is **terminal** (the same input cannot succeed) and its message is shown as the instruction it is; anything else is terminal with a generic sentence and a pointer to diagnostics, never its raw message; an `AggregateException` is classified by what it wraps. `Describe(error, retryAvailable, sourceRoute, sourceLabel)` builds the model: retryable → warning + "Geçici hata", terminal → error + "İşlem yapılamadı"; **"Yeniden dene" is offered only when the failure is retryable *and* the surface can actually re-run the action** — a retry button with nothing behind it is a lie; diagnostics is always offered; "go to source" only when the surface said where the fix lives. `Merge(standing, incoming)` turns a duplicate (same recovery, same text) into a count on the standing banner, upgrading it to retryable if the later occurrence can be retried. Text passes the raw-payload check (#815) and the central sanitizer and is capped. `Create(model, retry, navigate, dismiss)` renders glyph + title + text (+ `×N`), a `WrapPanel` of focusable actions (so a narrow window wraps them instead of clipping), Escape inside the banner dismisses, and an automation name with severity, text and count; errors are assertive live regions. `ErrorSurface(host, navigate)` is one surface per workspace: at most one banner, duplicates merged, `Show(error, retry, sourceRoute, sourceLabel)` / `Clear()`.

Wired: `ChannelListingMatrixPanel.cs` and `DiagnosticsPanel.cs` — the shared `Button(errors, …)`/`AsyncButton(errors, …)` helpers now clear the surface before an action and report a failure into it (the async one hands the action back as the retry; the matrix points "go to source" at the connections screen); **no `MessageBox.Show` remains in either panel**. `DashboardPanel.cs` — a failed refresh raises a banner whose retry is `RefreshAsync(force: true)`, cleared on the next successful load, above the figures it could not refresh (#807's stale re-render still happens beneath it).

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ErrorBannerTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): four retryable exception types offering retry only with a re-runnable action and reading as warnings, the wrapper classified by its inner failure; validation shown as its instruction and an internal message replaced by a sentence, neither offering retry; a duplicate counted and upgraded, different news replacing; the source action only when a route is given; and a bearer token, e-mail, profile path, a JSON body and a 2000-character message never reaching the banner. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ErrorBannerControlTests.cs` (new, 1, in a real owned window): the four actions by automation name, all focusable; a repeat counted on the same banner; the banner measured at 900 vs 260 DIP needing more height (it wraps); go-to-source navigating; retry running the action and clearing; a terminal banner without retry; Escape inside the banner handled and the host collapsing.

A trap this branch hit and recorded: the first version of the control test built a bare `HwndSource` only to construct a `KeyEventArgs`; the STA thread exited without disposing it, and when a later test pumped messages the orphaned window's WndProc fired on a dead thread and killed the test host (`NullReferenceException` in `HwndSubclass.SubclassWndProc`) — the suite reported "50 passed, Test Run Aborted". The focused run had passed only because the host exited first. The test now hosts the banner in a real `Window` and closes it. The lesson kept in the working notes: a suite summary line is not evidence unless its total matches the last green run.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ErrorBanner.cs (new), ChannelListingMatrixPanel.cs, DiagnosticsPanel.cs, DashboardPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ErrorBannerTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ErrorBannerControlTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=422 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 416; an earlier run of this branch aborted at 50 because of the test-side HwndSource above and was not used as evidence); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 0a9e89bf9d513c8c44eee9a096c5ae6b2450ab2317dac7f88faa32dfe1b09d96.
Not done: the other panels with their own `MessageBox`/status-text error paths (`OrdersPanel`, `BulkProductsPanel`, `DataBackupPanel`, the channel panels…) are not yet on an `ErrorSurface` — each is a copy of the same helper pattern and the change is mechanical, but every one is its own screen to verify; the `MainWindow`'s own `Log(Safe(e), Error)` sites (#814) raise toasts, not banners, so a failure in the product workspace still has no retry action; retry re-runs the whole action with no backoff and no attempt limit (a busy database retried instantly may be busy again — the banner simply reappears with a count); the source route for the matrix is a fixed "connections", not derived from which channel failed.

## #817 — warning versus blocking visual semantics (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-817-warning-vs-blocking-semantics off codex/issue-816-recoverable-error-banner

New issue. DESIGN: "warning" and "blocking" were drawn by five owners with their own glyphs and their own RGB literals (the validation panel, the anomaly cards, the error banner, the toasts, the row state), differing mostly in hue — which is the one channel a colour-blind operator or a high-contrast theme takes away.

`SeverityStyle.cs` (new): `SeverityLevel { Info, Success, Warning, Blocking }` ordered so "the highest wins" is a comparison; `For(level, highContrast)` returns a `SeverityPresentation` in which a warning and a blocking error differ **in every channel at once** — glyph (⚠ vs ✖), word ("Uyarı" vs "Hata"), border weight (1 vs 2), call to action ("Gözden geçir" vs "Düzelt") — and only last in colour. Under high contrast the accents are `SystemColors` (HotTrack for blocking, Highlight for warning) and the surface is the system window colour; the RGB table is never consulted. `AccentBrush`/`SurfaceBrush` are frozen. `FromValidation`/`FromNotification`/`FromAnomaly` map the existing owners' vocabularies (an unknown validation severity is informational, never silently blocking). `Aggregate(findings)` rolls a mixed set up: highest level, blocking and warning counted separately, a headline that leads with the blocking count and the first blocking message ("2 engel · 1 uyarı — önce engeller. Ürün adı zorunlu."), warnings alone saying "kaydedilebilir", and **one** call to action that belongs to the blocking findings ("İlk engele git") — a surface never invites a "save anyway" past a blocker. A message that looks like a raw payload (#815) is replaced before it becomes a headline; the headline passes the sanitizer and is trimmed.

Wired: `MainWindow.Filters.cs` `ShowProductValidation` — headline, colour and the single CTA come from the aggregate; every finding row prints `Badge + " · " + message` with the level's accent, high-contrast aware. `DashboardPanel.cs` anomaly cards — glyph, accent and border weight from the style. `ErrorBanner.Create` and `MainWindow.Notifications.cs` `RenderToasts` — glyph, word, accent, surface and border weight from the style (the banner's word for an error is now the same "Hata" the toast used). `ProductRowState.AccentBrush` — its error and warning accents are the shared table's, so the row and the panel cannot drift (pending/stale/disabled keep their own hues, which the shared table has no level for).

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SeverityStyleTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): warning vs blocking differing in glyph, word, border weight, CTA and accent, with every level having its own glyph and word; high contrast keeping the non-colour signals and switching to system colours, brushes frozen; a mixed set rolling up to blocking with counts, headline and CTA, warnings-only saying "kaydedilebilir", an empty set having no CTA; the mappings agreeing with the owners; and a JSON body, a bearer token, an e-mail and a 900-character finding never becoming a headline. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SeverityStyleWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): the real product card with a blocking and a warning finding — a "✖ Hata · …" row, a headline with the blocking count, a single focusable "İlk engele git" button; after fixing the blocker, no blocking badge and no blocking CTA.

A trap this branch hit and recorded: the window test edited the product draft and then closed the window; `MainWindow.OnClosing` → `ResolveProductEdit()` asks about unsaved changes, and that modal question in a test host waits forever — the tests all passed, the host never exited, the DLL stayed locked and every following build failed with MSB3027, which for a while looked like more hangs. Diagnosed with a step file plus `--blame-hang`; the test now restores the draft before closing.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=SeverityStyle.cs (new), MainWindow.Filters.cs, DashboardPanel.cs, ErrorBanner.cs, MainWindow.Notifications.cs, ProductRowState.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SeverityStyleTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SeverityStyleWindowTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=428 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 422); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 70cc31cdf325329b2173b1250cce5728818436edf7a2857df9d44630e11856cd.
Not done: the product card's own field-level warnings (price summary, stock summary, provenance, content diff — four "⚠ " literals in `MainWindow.Filters.cs`) still colour themselves; they are one-line changes each but each is its own surface to look at, and this branch consolidated the five owners that decide *severity*, not every place that prints a warning glyph. There is no shared XAML `Style` resource — the standard lives in code because every consumer builds its controls in code; a XAML consumer would need a converter. High contrast is read at render time and not re-rendered on a live theme switch (same as #794). The "keyboard" claim rests on the CTA being a focusable `Button` in the tab order; no test presses Tab through the panel.

## #818 — resizable dialog layout standard (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-818-resizable-dialog-standard off codex/issue-817-warning-vs-blocking-semantics

New issue. DESIGN: every code-built dialog sized itself — 520×700 for the Excel mapping, 720×560 for search results, 980×720 for the wizard — with no fit rule, so a 1024×768 screen or a 200 % DPI laptop got a dialog taller than the work area; some were `NoResize`, most had no minimum, buttons were part of the scrolled body or absent, and the delete confirmation was a `MessageBox` whose Enter meant "Yes".

`DialogLayout.cs` (new): `DialogLayout.Fit(width, height, workAreaWidth, workAreaHeight)` decides size in DIPs — clamped to the work area minus a 48-DIP margin, never below 320×240, `NaN` treated as "no request" — and returns min/max as well, so 200 % DPI is simply a smaller DIP work area with no pixel conversion anywhere. `DialogShell.Create(owner, title, body, actions, w, h, destructive)` is the one dialog window: sanitized title, body in a vertical `ScrollViewer` (horizontal disabled, so text wraps), a button bar **docked under the body and never scrolled away**, every action a focusable named button, the primary action on Enter and the cancel action on Escape — **reversed for a destructive dialog so a stray Enter cannot confirm** — `CanResize` between the fitted min and max, `CenterOwner`. `DialogShell.Message(text)` is the wrapped, sanitized message body; `DialogShell.Confirm(owner, title, message, confirmLabel)` is the standard confirmation. **Finding fixed on the way:** `DialogLayout.SafeText` first used `AuditStore.Sanitize`, whose pipeline caps text (2 000 characters, and `MarketplaceConnectionStore.Redact` inside it at 500) — right for a stored audit row, wrong for a modal, where a long validation message must be read in full. `AuditStore.Redact` (uncapped) and `MarketplaceConnectionStore.RedactSecrets` (uncapped) now exist; `Sanitize` and `Redact` keep their old contracts byte-for-byte on top of them; the dialog uses the uncapped path.

Converted to the shell: the global search results (`MainWindow.UX.cs`), the content-change preview and the column chooser (`MainWindow.Filters.cs` — the chooser's Kaydet is now Enter and Vazgeç is Escape, with its save logic as the action), the unsaved-changes prompt (`MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs` — destructive, so Enter stays on İptal exactly as before), the product delete confirmation (`MainWindow.xaml.cs` — `Confirm(…, "Sil")`, Enter is now Vazgeç), the Excel column-mapping dialog (`MainWindow.Excel.cs` — wrapped in the shell with its inline "Eşlemeyi kullan" logic intact), and the onboarding wizard (`OnboardingPanel.cs` — sized through `Fit`, so it opens at 1024×768). Two fixtures that drove the old prompt by casting its content to `StackPanel` (`ProductCardPriceFieldsUiTests`, `ProductDirtyIndicatorUiTests`) and one helper (`ProductCardDirtyDraftTests.Answer`) now cast to `Panel`, which fits both layouts; without that the prompt stayed open and the host hung in teardown — found with `--blame-hang`, the way #817's trap taught.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DialogLayoutTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): a 520×700 request fitting a 1024×728 work area with the height capped; a 720×560 request at 960×540 DIPs (200 % DPI) fitted by the same rule; tiny, `NaN` and absurd-screen requests lifted to usable sizes; a title carrying a bearer token, an e-mail and a profile path redacted; and **a 5 500-character validation message redacted but not truncated** (the test that exposed the cap). `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DialogShellTests.cs` (new, 1, in a real owned window): sanitized title, `CanResize`, min sizes, max height within the work area, long text not widening the dialog, the body scrolling (`ScrollableHeight > 0`) with the bar docked outside the scroll, primary `IsDefault` and cancel `IsCancel`, the primary action running and closing, and a destructive dialog putting Enter on Vazgeç.

## #819 — form label and help-text alignment (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-819-form-label-help-alignment off codex/issue-818-resizable-dialog-standard

New issue. DESIGN: ten panels each had their own `Field(panel, label)` helper with its own label margin and colour; none marked a required field, none had a help line, none had a place for a validation message, and a long Turkish label was a single unwrapped `TextBlock`. The product and source forms shared one `Field(panel, label, property)` (38 rows) that added a bare label and the input as siblings.

`FormField.cs` (new): `FormFieldSpec(Label, Required, Help)` and `FormField.Build(spec, input)` → `FormFieldControl` — one row shape: the label above (wrapping, quiet colour), the required marker appended for the eye **and** the word "zorunlu" in the input's accessible name for the ear (`AutomationProperties.Name` + `LabeledBy`), help text under the input in the small quiet style (collapsed when empty, and spoken as the input's `HelpText`), and a validation slot under that, collapsed until `SetValidation(message, level)` fills it with #817's glyph and accent and adds the message to the field's spoken help; `SetValidation("")` clears and restores the plain help. Spacing is three DIP constants (`LabelGap`, `RowGap`, `HelpGap`), so 100–200 % DPI is the same rhythm. `SafeHelp` is the security line: help is copy, not a specimen — a line that shows a credential as an example ("Örn: api_key=…", "token: …", "Bearer …", "Parola: …") is **refused outright**, checked on the raw text before redaction (redaction would have rewritten the specimen into "[redacted]=[redacted]" and hidden it — the test caught that ordering), and what remains passes the uncapped `AuditStore.Redact` (#818).

Wired: `MainWindow.xaml.cs` `Field(panel, label, property)` — every product and source text row now builds through `FormField`, with `RequiredFor` (Name, Currency: the fields `ProductValidation`'s blocking rules refuse a save without) and `HelpFor` (Name, Sku, Barcode, Currency, Price, Cost, Stock) as two small tables next to it; `Label(...)` stays for the combo/other callers it still has. `OnboardingPanel.AddField` builds through `FormField` too. Three card fixtures that took inputs as direct children of the editor now look one row level deep.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FormFieldTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): required as marker + word, optional as neither, the marker trailing; help normalised and five specimen credentials refused, PII redacted; the built row's four parts in order with `LabeledBy`, spoken name, spoken help and a collapsed validation slot, DIP row margin, no empty help line; a long Turkish label measured at 700 vs 220 DIP wrapping to more lines and never asking for more width than the row; a validation message appearing with the blocking glyph and spoken on the field, redacted, and clearing back to the plain help. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FormFieldWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): the real product card — every bound text input labeled for assistive tech, the name field's label ending in the marker and its spoken name in "zorunlu", the description not called required.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=FormField.cs (new), MainWindow.xaml.cs, OnboardingPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FormFieldTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FormFieldWindowTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductCardDirtyDraftTests.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductCardPriceFieldsUiTests.cs, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=440 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 434); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 758d06bb480ddd1eb5ef808d2ef85d7322e50f15bc5a1a810a4f80a5b83f90e4.
Not done: the nine channel/tool panels' own `Field` helpers (Allegro, Amazon, eBay, Fruugo, Hepsiburada, Joom, Message, Orders, Locale…) are not on `FormField` yet — each is a two-line change, each its own screen to look at; the validation slot exists on every product/source row but nothing writes to it yet — that is #820's form-level summary, which will own field focus and per-field messages; required-ness is a fixed table beside `Field`, not derived from `ProductValidation` at runtime (the two agree today and a test could pin that); `Label(...)` rows for combo boxes keep the old plain label; DPI is asserted through DIP constants, not a rendered 200 % test.

## #820 — form-level validation summary (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-820-form-validation-summary off codex/issue-819-form-label-help-alignment

New issue. DESIGN: a save with a bad record was refused by the store with one sentence in the status line; nothing at the top of the form listed every problem, nothing under the inputs said which ones, and nothing moved focus to the first field to fix. The summary panel from #803/#817 existed only as a passive view bound on selection, and its call to action jumped to a *section*, not to a field.

`FormValidationSummary.cs` (new, pure): `Compose(result, propertyByField, enteredValues)` turns `ProductValidation`'s findings into `FormValidationLink`s — blocking first in the form's own section order, warnings after, information left out — each linked to the input that fixes it through `ProductPropertyByField` (a finding whose field the form does not own is listed **without** a focus target rather than dropped, and still blocks); `FirstBlocking` is the input the save focuses; `CanSave` is "no blocker". `Mask` is the security line: a rule message is about the rule, and if a message quotes what the operator typed (any entered value of four characters or more), the quote is masked before the summary; `ProductValidation`'s real messages never echo a value, and a test pins both.

Wired: `MainWindow.xaml.cs` — `Field` (#819) now registers every row it builds in `formRows` keyed by (form, property), so a form can write under an input and focus it. `MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs` — `SaveProductEdit` evaluates the whole record first, `ApplyFormValidation` writes each link's message under its input (blocking or warning style) and clears the rest, `ShowProductValidation` refreshes the top summary, and a blocker **focuses the first blocking input and refuses the save** with the aggregate headline (the store would refuse the same record, but without saying where); a successful save clears every message. `FocusField` selects the section that actually holds the input by walking the **logical** tree up to its `TabItem` (a tab's content is presented by the TabControl's ContentPresenter, so the visual chain skips the TabItem and lands on the outer route page — the first version did exactly that, and the window test's diagnostics caught it), then focuses now and again at `Loaded` priority, because a freshly selected tab refuses focus until its next layout pass. `MainWindow.Filters.cs` — the summary's "İlk engele git" now focuses the first blocking **input**, falling back to the section. `SaveSource` — the source form's two rules (name, location) go through the same path: messages under the inputs, focus on the first, save refused with the headline.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FormValidationSummaryTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): the first blocking finding in form order is the focus target with the "İlk engele git" call to action; findings across sections all listed, every blocker before every warning, no information; every blocking rule the store enforces resolves to an input the form can focus (pins the field map against `ProductValidation`); a fixed record clears the summary and allows the save; a crafted message quoting a typed value is masked in the link and the headline while an empty value masks nothing, and the real rules never echo; a finding the form does not own is listed without focus and still blocks. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FormValidationWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): the real product card with the name emptied and the currency set to "SECRET1234" — the save throws, the store is untouched, the summary is visible, both rows carry their own message in two sections, the typed value appears nowhere, keyboard focus is on the name input; after fixing both, the save goes through, both slots collapse, the store has "TRY". Its teardown auto-answers the unsaved-changes prompt so a failed assertion reports instead of hanging.

Two things fixed on the way, both honest: #819's CI run was red on a pre-existing timing test (`XmlSourceHealthCheckerTests`: a 30 ms artificial delay measured as 29 ms on the runner — `Task.Delay` and the stopwatch do not share a clock edge); its lower bound is now 25 ms and the assertion still proves the latency was measured, committed on #819 as 0dc46d9 so this branch has a green base. And the section-focus bug above, which would have sent an operator to the wrong tab.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=FormValidationSummary.cs (new), MainWindow.xaml.cs, MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs, MainWindow.Filters.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FormValidationSummaryTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FormValidationWindowTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=447 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 440); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 a02c21b95e24fe252d3b7a9fe8cbfbe2ce20802084b3587d467abfe6e72cc39c.
Not done: the settings forms are not on this path — none of the settings panels build rows through `Field`, so they have no registry to write to (that follows the panel-by-panel `FormField` adoption left from #819); the source form's rules are the two the old code had (name, location) written inline in `SaveSource`, not a `SourceValidation` rule set like the product's — a rule owner for sources is its own issue; warnings are shown under their inputs but nothing lets the operator "acknowledge" them, and the save proceeds past them silently, which is the intended policy but has no affordance; the numeric-binding errors that `ValidBindings` throws for ("Kırmızı işaretli sayı alanlarını düzelt.") are raised before the summary and are not yet folded into it; keyboard is asserted through real focus in one window test, not through Tab traversal from the summary button.

## #821 — destructive action typed confirmation (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-821-destructive-typed-confirmation off codex/issue-820-form-validation-summary

New issue. DESIGN: "Pasife al" on a multi-selection and "Önizlemeyi onayla ve uygula" on a bulk preview were Yes/No boxes — answered by reflex, with the affected count in the sentence but nothing that made the operator read it, and nothing that noticed when the count had moved between the preview and the click.

`DestructiveConfirmation.cs` (new): `DestructiveIntent(Verb, Scope, AffectedCount, IsLocalOnly, Noun)`; from **two** records on the count must be typed (`RequiresTyping`), a single record keeps #818's ordinary destructive confirm; the phrase is the count itself as digits — language-neutral, and it forces the number to be read; `Validate(intent, typed, currentCount)` allows only the exact phrase (surrounding whitespace forgiven), **refuses a count that moved since the dialog opened with both numbers** ("12 → 9; yeniden önizleyip tekrar onaylayın") because the operator confirmed what they saw, and nothing typed is Cancel. The security line is first in the rule order: an intent that is not local-only is refused outright with the reason that a live marketplace write needs preview, explicit approval, revision, idempotency and the wrong-store guard — a typed word never stands in for them — and `DestructiveConfirmDialog.Show` throws before it even opens for such an intent. `DestructiveConfirmDialog.Build/Show` puts the pattern on the #818 shell: count, scope and verb in the message, a text box, the destructive button **disabled and not Enter until the phrase matches** (so Enter cannot confirm by reflex; Escape always cancels), and at the click the count is taken again from the caller's `currentCount()` — a stale count shows its reason inside the dialog, disables the button and keeps the dialog open to be read.

Wired: `MainWindow.xaml.cs` `SetProductActive(false)` — deactivating two or more selected products asks for the typed count, re-counted from the grid's live selection at the click; activation is not destructive and asks nothing; the single-product delete keeps `Confirm`. `BulkProductsPanel.cs` — the apply confirmation is the typed count of READY lines, **re-counted at the click by re-running the preview against the store with the same selection rule the preview used** (selection, else all), so a product edited or removed since the preview changes the number and the click refuses. The message still says the write is local and no live marketplace write happens.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DestructiveConfirmationTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): phrase = count and the message naming count, scope, verb and irreversibility, one record needing no typing; only the exact phrase accepted (seven wrong texts refused, each reason naming the phrase); a moved count refused with both numbers, more as wrong as fewer; nothing typed is Cancel and a single record still has its count checked; a live-write intent refused by `Validate` and by the dialog. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DestructiveConfirmDialogTests.cs` (new, 1, real owned window): the button dead and not default until "40" is typed, a partial "4" not waking it, a count that moves to 37 at the click keeping the dialog open with "37" in the reason and the button dead again, and the retyped phrase with a steady count closing it confirmed.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=DestructiveConfirmation.cs (new), MainWindow.xaml.cs, BulkProductsPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DestructiveConfirmationTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DestructiveConfirmDialogTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=453 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 447); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 553480372ea0e90dca60cbbb134c53fbcee45bf61e65a234819072f987920a17.
Not done: the other high-impact local commands (source delete, data-backup restore, migration cleanup, taxonomy merges, order-exception bulk decisions) are not on the pattern — each is a one-call change at its confirmation site plus a real re-count function; the bulk-products path is exercised through the dialog and pure tests, not through a panel-level test (the panel is a static closure and its confirm is modal); the typed phrase is the count only — no "type the store name" variant, which the wrong-store guard for live writes would need and which is deliberately out of this pattern's scope; the threshold of two is a constant, not a per-command setting.

## #822 — import workflow stepper (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-822-import-workflow-stepper off codex/issue-821-destructive-typed-confirmation

New issue. DESIGN: the XML import was a page of buttons — "XML'i oku", "Önizleme hesapla", "Havuza aktar" — whose order and preconditions lived in exception messages ("önce XML'i oku", "önizleme geçersiz"); the flow's real state (an XML read for this address, a mapping the catalogue accepts, a preview whose fingerprint still matches, a run in progress) was there but invisible until a button refused.

`ImportStepper.cs` (new, pure): `ImportFlowState` is the window's real state as data — source chosen and saved, XML loaded and for the current address, mapping ready (or its problem), preview existing and **fresh** (fingerprint matches the XML and the mapping), apply running/cancelled, result status/failed. `Compute(state)` gives six stages — Kaynak, Eşleme, Doğrulama, Önizleme, Havuza aktar (yerel), Sonuç — each with a status (pending/current/done/blocked/stale/running), whether it can be jumped to, and a reason. The rules are the ones the commands already enforce, now visible before the click: back is always allowed; forward only when the state behind the stage is real (no read → no preview; failed mapping → validation blocked and nothing past it; stale preview → apply pending with "yeniden önizleyin"); a running apply cannot be jumped; a cancelled or failed apply shows the reason at Sonuç. `Current(state)` is the first stage not yet done; `Restart` keeps the source and forgets everything after it. No stage is a fake percentage and **no stage is a live marketplace write** — `IsLiveWrite` is false for all six and the apply label says "yerel"; downstream channel writes keep their own approval guards untouched.

Wired: `MainWindow.xaml.cs` — `CurrentImportFlowState()` reads the real fields (`source`, `xml`, `loadedLocation`, `previewRevision` against `XmlPreviewFingerprint.Create`, `previewMappingShapeFingerprint` against a fresh `MappingSnapshot`, `EnsureMappingReady` for readiness with its message as the problem, `LastStatus` for the result) plus three run flags set around the import (`importRunning`, `importCancelled` on `OperationCanceledException`, `importFailed`); `RefreshImportStepper()` renders the strip as focusable chips — glyph + label, enabled only when jumpable, reason as tooltip, a spoken name "Önizleme: geçersiz. XML veya eşleme değişti…" — and is called from `SetSource`, at the end of `InspectAsync` and `PreviewAsync`, and at the start and in a new `finally` of `ImportAsync`; a chip jump focuses the control that owns the stage (source list, mapping grid, preview grid). The strip sits at the top of the source column.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportStepperTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): forward allowed only on real state (no source / unread / read-and-mapped); back always allowed with earlier stages reading done; a mapping failure blocking validation with its reason and everything after it; a stale preview shown stale, jumpable, blocking apply; running, cancelled, failed and done applies reading honestly and restart returning to the read; no live-write stage, the apply label saying "yerel", six stages in enum order. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportStepperWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): the real XML page with a saved source and no read — six focusable chips with spoken states, source ✔, mapping ✖ with "okunmadı", preview and apply disabled, apply labelled "yerel".

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ImportStepper.cs (new), MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportStepperTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportStepperWindowTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=460 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 453); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 23f19b8ecef57d9677c1310a06e976eccaae92ac953a9f88b5c31b8fae7661c5.
Not done: the strip is not refreshed on a mapping cell edit — it catches the resulting staleness at the next event (`SetSource`, read, preview, import), not on the keystroke; `CurrentImportFlowState` re-runs `MappingSnapshot` over the loaded XML on every refresh, which is what `PreviewAsync` does anyway but is not free on a very large feed; there is no "Yeniden başla" chip — restart is modelled (`Restart`) but not offered as an action; the Excel import path has its own flow and no stepper; jumps focus a control, they do not run the stage (a stale preview chip focuses the preview grid; the operator still presses "Önizleme hesapla").

## #823 — import source selection clarity (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-823-import-source-selection off codex/issue-822-import-workflow-stepper

New issue. DESIGN: the XML page's source list was a list of names — no sign of what kind of source each was, whether this build could even read it, whether it was switched off, how it had fared last time, or which one the operator used a minute ago; "+ Yeni XML kaynağı" created a source of one shape regardless of what the build supports, and a location with `?token=…` in it was shown as typed.

`ImportSourceCatalog.cs` (new, pure): `Classify(location, fileExists)` is **the reader's own rule, mirrored without reading** — a file that exists, or an absolute https address with no user information; everything else is unsupported and says why (http/ftp → "yalnız HTTPS", `user:pass@` → "kullanıcı bilgisi desteklenmiyor; Basic Auth alanlarını kullanın", a path to nothing → "dosya bulunamadı", garbage → "geçerli bir HTTPS adresi veya dosya yolu değil"). `SupportedTypes(routeExists)` is the picker: XML by address, XML by file, and Excel **only when the Excel screen is registered** — a type is never invented. `MaskLocation` rebuilds an address with user information as "[user]@" and secret query parameters (`token`, `key`, `sig`, `auth`, `password`, …) as "[redacted]" while host, port, path and harmless parameters survive, and redacts a profile path like everywhere else. `Describe` builds a row — title (an unnamed source still gets one), kind label, supported, enabled, last-used, a health label from the source's own persisted fields ("ready · son başarı 5 dk önce", "hiç başarılı olmadı", "hiç denenmedi"), masked location, reason — and `Options` orders rows the way the operator needs them: last-used first, then usable, then disabled, then unsupported, alphabetical within.

Wired: `MainWindow.xaml.cs` — the `sources` list drops `DisplayMemberPath` for an item template whose text is the row summary ("★ Beta · XML adresi", "Kapalı · XML adresi · pasif", "Eski · desteklenmiyor · Yalnız HTTPS…"), whose tooltip is the masked location plus the reason, and whose automation name is the summary; `RefreshSources` computes the rows (health from `LastFeedState`/`LastSuccessfulFeedUtc`, support through `File.Exists`), orders the `XmlSource` items by them (selection code still receives `XmlSource`) and preselects the last-used one; `SetSource` records the last-used id under `xml:last-source` in `UiPreferenceStore` for a saved source; the single "+ Yeni XML kaynağı" button became a type picker (`sourceTypePicker`, refreshed from the window's real route table on open) plus "+ Yeni kaynak" — an XML type creates the source with the type's hint logged, Excel navigates to the Excel screen.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportSourceCatalogTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): support mirroring the reader with reasons for http, user-info, ftp, a missing file, garbage and empty; a location with user info and four secret parameters masked while host, port, path and `v=2` survive, and a profile path redacted; the picker offering Excel only with the Excel route and no credential example in any hint; rows ordered last-used → usable → disabled → unsupported with the marks and reasons in their summaries and "hiç denenmedi" without health; health labels for ready/failed/none and a nameless source. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportSourceListWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): the real XML page with four saved sources and a last-used preference — order, preselection, the rendered rows' marks, "pasif", "desteklenmiyor" with "HTTPS" in the tooltip, a `token=SECRET123` never in text or tooltip while the address stays recognisable, and the picker listing exactly XML address, XML file and Excel.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ImportSourceCatalog.cs (new), MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportSourceCatalogTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportSourceListWindowTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=466 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 460); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 ec2884b30a6e8de2e4d2f0c7239183cef03f15650455c1961390bc25faa2c2a1.
Not done: the health label is the source's persisted fields, not a live reachability probe — a fresh check still comes from the page's existing summary after selection; choosing "XML dosyası" creates an empty source rather than opening a file picker; the list is not grouped by store/health (that is #829); an unsupported saved source is listed and labelled rather than hidden, deliberately — hiding a record the operator saved would look like data loss; DPI is asserted through wrapping rows, not a rendered 200 % test.

## #824 — import mapping table readability (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-824-import-mapping-readability off codex/issue-823-import-source-selection

New issue. DESIGN: the mapping grid had two columns — the catalogue field and an XML path combo — and nothing else: no sign which fields the import cannot start without, what type a field expects, what the chosen path actually holds in the supplier's file, or that two catalogue fields had been pointed at the same path. The operator learned all of that from the exception at preview time.

`ImportMappingTable.cs` (new, pure): `Compose(rows, knownPaths, sampleFor)` gives every target field a readable row — `Required` (with a label built from the catalogue's real rule, which is a **group**: a product needs a SKU *or* a barcode, and a name, exactly as `XmlCatalog.RequiredFields` enforces, so the label reads "✱ zorunlu (veya barkod)" and an empty SKU beside a mapped barcode is not a problem), a type label per field (ondalık sayı, tam sayı, URL listesi, kod, 3 harfli kod, uzun metin, metin), a **sample** of the chosen path's value, and a status with a reason: mapped; empty-optional; missing-required (every empty member of an unsatisfied group, the way the snapshot reports it); duplicate target (one XML path on two catalogue fields — flagged on both, naming the path; several image paths on one field are not a conflict); unknown path (a path the scan did not list — never flagged before a scan has run, since then there is no list to be absent from). The summary counts match the rows and ends in "eşleme hazır" or "önce sorunları düzeltin"; `FirstProblemKey` is the row to jump to. Samples are untrusted supplier data: through the uncapped redaction, a raw body replaced, cut to one short line. No XML variant mapping was added.

Wired: `MainWindow.xaml.cs` — `MappingEntry` gained five computed properties; the `mapping` grid gained "Zorunlu", "Tür", "Örnek (ilk kayıt)" and "Durum" columns, a row tooltip carrying the reason that also opens on keyboard focus, and a recompute after every cell edit; a summary line and an "İlk soruna git" button (selects, scrolls to and focuses the first problem row) sit above the grid; after a read, the first item of the scanned XML is kept as the sample source and samples are resolved by the same relative-path rule the import uses (`a/b`, `@attr`); the table is recomputed when a source is bound and after every scan.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportMappingTableTests.cs` (new, 7, red first): required/type/sample/status readable on every row with a ready summary; a missing required field named with its reason and first; SKU-or-barcode as one requirement (labels, barcode satisfying an empty SKU, neither mapped flagging both); one path on two targets a conflict on both while several image paths are not; an unknown path flagged after a scan and not before; samples masked, capped, and a JSON body replaced; 152 fields composing with counts that match the rows. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportMappingWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): the real XML page reading a local two-record file — "✱ zorunlu (veya barkod)" on SKU, "ABC-1" as its sample, a masked description sample, "uzun metin", the unmapped name flagged ✖ with "Zorunlu", the summary and the readable columns present, and "İlk soruna git" selecting that row.
