## #864 — DataGrid text truncation and tooltip policy (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-864-grid-text-policy off codex/issue-863-form-control-heights (e1ee939)

One text column standard for every code-built grid. `GridColumns.cs` (new): `GridTextKind` (Text, Identifier, Number) and `GridColumns.Text(header, path, width, kind?, format?)`, the one way to declare a text column — prose (a name, a source, a status, an error) sits on one line and is trimmed with an ellipsis; while it is trimmed, and only then, its cell carries a tooltip with the full text, which the keyboard opens on the focused cell as the mouse does on hover; an identifier (a SKU, a barcode, an id, a tracking number) or a number (a price, a stock, a count, a rate) is never trimmed and never gets a tooltip. `KindFor(path)` guesses from the bound path's words (a "...Label" is prose however it is named; the report grid guesses from the column key), and a caller can say the kind outright. The tooltip passes the central redaction (`GridTooltipConverter`: a token, an address or a raw payload never opens in a bubble; an empty cell gets none). The framework on .NET 8 exposes no trimmed flag, so the prose text block measures its own trimming (the full text's width against its own) on size change, load and rebinding (a recycled row keeps its block and only rebinds it), and hands the cell its tooltip or clears it so a grid's own tooltip comes back. The column adds nothing to the cell's style, so a grid's density, selection and focus styles stay in force. Migrated: the main window's `Column` helper (keeps the N2/N4 formats), the dashboard's `AddColumn`, the sync history and XML run grids, the orders grid, the channel matrix's SKU and product columns, the channel products grid, the XML mapping grid's read-only columns, the report result grid, and 26 inline column declarations across 15 panels.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/GridColumnsTests.cs` (new, 1, red first — the red build showed the framework has no `IsTextTrimmed`, so the measured trimming replaced the planned trigger): the kind guesses; the prose column's trimming, wrapping and watch setters and no cell style of its own; the identifier and number columns never watched; the format; the converter redacting a bearer value and an address, hiding a JSON payload, and returning nothing for empty; a real grid with a long Turkish and Japanese title in a 120-DIP column trimmed with a redacted keyboard-openable tooltip, the identifier beside it whole, and the tooltip gone once the column is widened. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/GridColumnsUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window — the product grid's name column at 90 DIP trims a long name and carries it whole in a keyboard-openable cell tooltip, the SKU column never trims, widening takes the tooltip away; the dashboard's connections grid trims its status the same way.

## #865 — Numeric column alignment standard (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-865-numeric-alignment off codex/issue-864-grid-text-policy (3f57798)

The number side of the shared text column. `GridColumns.cs`: a Number column (`GridTextKind.Number`, by the caller or by `KindFor`) is right-aligned with tabular figures (`Typography.NumeralAlignment`), so every digit takes the same width and the digits line up down the column; its decimals follow `DefaultFormat(path)` unless the caller says otherwise — a count (stock, count, quantity) none, money (price, cost, total, amount, fee, margin, and a FormulaPriceTry) two places, anything else as the value comes (a rate keeps its caller's N4); the column carries `GridColumns.IsNumeric`, and the token file's shared `DataGridColumnHeader` style aligns such a header right (`Themes/DesignTokens.xaml`: a `DataTrigger` through `NumericHeaderConverter` on the header and its `DisplayIndex`, because a header's `Column` is a plain property attached after the header enters the tree and a binding on it never re-evaluates). A negative keeps its sign, zero is written out, a large value carries the culture's group separators, and a currency is never a symbol pasted into the number: it is its own text column. `KindFor` now decides by the bound path's last word first — a "...Label", "...Source", "...Currency", "...Status", "...Utc" is prose however it starts (PriceSource, CostCurrency, UpdatedUtc were being read as numbers), a last number or identifier word settles it (TotalCount, OrderId), then any word (FormulaPriceTry, TrackingNumbers); the order-line quantities (Ordered, Shipped, Returned, Cancelled, Outstanding) and the dashboard's Orders count are numbers. Defect fixed on the way: a WPF binding formats with the element's xml:lang (en-US unless a window says otherwise), not the thread's culture, so the product grid's price column read "1,234.50" on this Turkish machine beside "1.234,50" everywhere else — the shared column sets the binding's `ConverterCulture` to the current culture. The numbers are device-independent.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NumericColumnsTests.cs` (new, 1, red first — the first green run caught the en-US binding culture and then the header trigger that never fired): the formats by kind; the column shape (right alignment, tabular figures, the numeric mark, the default and an explicit format; prose and identifiers left with no format); a real grid under the shared styles with a negative, a zero and a large value across three currencies — the texts in the current culture, no currency symbol, the currency in its own column, right edges on the cell's right padding edge and equal across rows, prose starting at the left, the price and stock headers right and the prose headers left — and the same DIP numbers under a 2× layout scale. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NumericColumnsUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window's product grid — price two decimals and stock none in the current culture, right-aligned tabular digits ending on the cell's right edge and lining up across rows, right-aligned price and stock headers, name and SKU left.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=3; FILES_CHANGED=GridColumns.cs, Themes/DesignTokens.xaml, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NumericColumnsTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NumericColumnsUiTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=619 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (Release; last green 617 + 2 new); artifact SHA-256 d772f76f04549fcd59a89baa199925be3f34d7b3bb76232e1ba722c4c7f002aa (publish/win-x64/TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.exe, self-contained win-x64 Release, not committed).

Not done: a percent, a rate or a duration has no default decimals (it shows as the value comes, or as its caller formats it); a preformatted money string (the orders grid's TotalLabel "1.234,50 TRY") is prose and stays left — a typed amount plus a currency column would bring it under the standard; the window's xml:lang is still en-US, so a binding-formatted number outside the shared column (none in a grid today) would keep en-US separators; tabular figures depend on the face (Segoe UI carries them, a fallback face may not); a negative is a plain minus (no colour, no parentheses); the header rule rides on the shared implicit header style (a grid with its own ColumnHeaderStyle would lose it — none today); the Excel and CSV exports' numeric formatting is theirs; DPI is checked by a 2× layout transform in the test host, not on a 150–200 % monitor.

## #866 — Date and time display consistency (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-866-time-display off codex/issue-865-numeric-alignment (bb0b071)

One owner for every timestamp a person reads. `TimeDisplay.cs` (new): the stores keep UTC and the display converts — `Format(utc[, zone][, missing])` is the local wall clock in the current culture's short form (`DateTime`, an unspecified kind read as UTC, or `DateTimeOffset`), `FormatDate` the day alone, `Offset(utc, zone)` the zone's offset at that instant ("UTC+03:00", "UTC-05:00", "UTC"), `Describe(utc, nowUtc[, zone])` the tooltip — local time with its offset, the age through `StatusTooltip.Relative`, and the UTC instant — and a time never recorded (null, default, MinValue) is `Missing` "—" with `MissingDescription` in words; `TimeDisplayConverter` and `TimeTooltipConverter` carry the same for bindings; `ColumnWidth` 155 is the grid column that fits the longest short form among tr-TR, en-US, de-DE and ja-JP at the body size. DST is the zone's own business: the spring hour that does not exist never shows, and the autumn overlap's two wall clocks are told apart by the offset in the tooltip. `GridColumns.cs`: `GridTextKind.Time` — a bound path ending in Utc, At, Date, Time, When or Timestamp is a time column: the value converted on the way in, tabular digits, an ellipsis if the user narrows the column, and the tooltip always there, on the text and (through `GridColumns.MonitorTooltip`) on the cell so the keyboard opens it. The sync centre's job grid (UpdatedUtc) and XML run grid (StartedUtc, FinishedUtc) and the connections form's LastValidatedUtc were binding raw UTC values straight to the cell — shown in UTC, in the en-US form a bare binding produces on a Turkish machine — and now show local time with the tooltip. Twenty-three ad-hoc local-time formats across sixteen files (alerts, API health, channel products, dashboard, Etsy readiness, automation, XML summary, connections, messages, order returns, the order sync label, orders detail and timeline, policy centre, production readiness, the status tooltip) go through the owner; the prose sites keep their own word for a missing time ("yok"), the grids and labels take the dash.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/TimeDisplayTests.cs` (new, 2, red first): missing in every shape and its words; UTC in, local out in a fixed zone (Turkey), for a DateTime, an unspecified kind and an offset value; the date alone; the tooltip's parts; offsets for Turkey, Bogotá and UTC; DST spring and autumn on Central European time — the non-existent hour never shown, the two 02:30s with different offsets and different tooltips; four cultures each equal to the culture's own short form and each fitting the shared column width; the order sync label and the converter; the time column's shape and a real grid — local text, no trimming at the shared width, the cell's tooltip equal to the description with the offset and the age and openable by keyboard, a dash and the missing words for a null. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/TimeDisplayUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window's sync centre — a queued job's update time and a failed XML run's start and end (a dash when it has none) as local text with the offset in the cell tooltip, and the dashboard's connections grid showing the dash for a connection never tested.

## #867 — Localization overflow regression (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-867-overflow-regression off codex/issue-866-time-display (d7ac2a6)

A regression suite that asks the layout itself, and the fixes it demanded. `OverflowAudit.cs` (new): `OverflowAudit.Audit(root)` walks the visual tree and reports every visible text block that is neither wrapped nor trimmed by design, and every button, tab item, column header, check box or combo box, that WPF had to clip because its natural size exceeded the slot it was given (`LayoutInformation.GetLayoutClip`, the framework's own verdict — width first, then height for a wrapped text in a fixed-height row); an `OverflowFinding` carries the kind, the owner (the nearest owning control with its name or tag, and the element), the text through the central redaction and capped at 60 characters, and the DIP it needed against the DIP it had. Wrapping, an ellipsis and a host that scrolls are not findings; a host that forbids scrolling is. The numbers are device-independent: the same tree under a 1.25×, 1.5× or 2× layout scale reports the same findings. What the first walk of the real window found, fixed at the seams: `GridColumns.cs` — a column is never narrower than its own header (`HeaderMinWidth`: the header's text in the header face, the header padding and the sort arrow's room, cached per header; the products grid was cutting "Alış döviz", "Satış döviz", "Medya kaynağı" and "Son veri kaynağı", the orders grid "Sipariş durumu (kaynak)"), and an identifier too long for its column ends in an ellipsis with the value whole in the tooltip (a refinement of #864's rule: a hard cut hides more than an ellipsis with a tooltip; a number is still never trimmed, it gets the room); the callers that set their own minimums (orders, matrix, report, channel products) combine them with the header's; `ChannelProductsPanel.cs` — the star-sized SKU, price and stock columns keep real minimums (120, 85, 75) so a 15-character Turkish SKU and a seven-digit price survive the window's minimum width; `MainWindow.xaml.cs` — both stock columns fit a seven-digit count (60 → 72).

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OverflowAuditTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): on a bare window under the shared styles — cut prose in a 100-DIP host found with the right DIP numbers, wrapped and trimmed text not found, a bearer value and an address never reaching a finding's text and the text capped, a 60-DIP button and an 80-DIP check box named as owners of their cut labels, a scrollable host not a finding and a host that forbids scrolling a finding, a 30-DIP number column found under its cell and a value that fits not found, and the same findings at 1.25×, 1.5× and 2×. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/LocalizationOverflowUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window with a long-named product, a long-named source, a failed XML run and a queued job — all 35 routes and every inner tab each route carries, at the default width and at the window's minimum, with no cut anywhere (the first run listed 33 cuts across products, orders and the seven channel product grids). `GridColumnsTests`/`GridColumnsUiTests` follow the identifier rule and the header minimum.

## #868 — Turkish casing-safe UI search (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-868-turkish-search off codex/issue-867-overflow-regression (58cdd51)

One fold for every display search. `UiSearch.cs` (new): `Fold(text)` normalizes to composed Unicode (a decomposed İ or ğ is the same letter), lower-cases by Turkish rules through `LocalCulture.Turkish` (İ is i, I is ı — an invariant lower-casing turns İ into i plus a combining dot and never matches), and then lets the dotted and the dotless i meet, so an ASCII keyboard that types I for ı and i for İ still finds the word; other diacritics stay significant (a çocuk is not a cocuk). `Matches(text, query)` is the folded substring test, and an empty or blank query matches everything; `text.ContainsFolded(query)` is the same in the shape the filters read. Wired into every display search: the product page's SQL search (`CatalogStore.Search`: `Open()` registers a deterministic SQLite function `uifold`, the five LIKE columns and the pattern go through it, so "istanbul", "ISTANBUL", "İSTANBUL" and "ıstanbul" all find "İstanbul Çorap" and a % or _ in the query stays a literal), the in-memory `CatalogStore.Products(search)`, the orders search, the sync centre's query, the media picker, the order exceptions search, the channel matrix query and its channel/shop filters, the taxonomy mapping search, the settings search, the source list filter, the sidebar filter, the report cards and the column chooser (every `CurrentCultureIgnoreCase` display comparison, which folds the i-family only under a Turkish culture and never forgives an ASCII I). Identity keys (a SKU, a barcode, an external key, a store key) keep their ordinal or NOCASE normalization; nothing on the write path changed. No search writes its query anywhere (a query can be personal data).

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/UiSearchTests.cs` (new, 2, red first): the i-family in eleven spellings both ways, mixed case, a decomposed İ and ğ, an emoji, other diacritics kept, the empty and blank query, a null text; the real product store — every spelling finds both İstanbul products through SQL, the dotless word is found dotted and undotted, the SKU still finds itself, a LIKE wildcard stays a literal, the in-memory filter agrees, the exact SKU is still the key; the report cards and the column chooser fold the same way; an address typed into the store, the products and the report search is in no audit event. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/UiSearchUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window — the product page search after its debounce, the orders search and the sync query, each with the four spellings and the empty query, and the audit trail clean of a typed address.

## #869 — Keyboard shortcut discoverability (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-869-shortcut-reference off codex/issue-868-turkish-search (e5ded24)

One catalogue, read by everything that mentions a key. `KeyboardShortcuts.cs` (new): `Shortcut(CommandKey, Label, Scope, Key, Modifiers, Condition, Destructive)` and the `Catalogue` of the shell's real commands — the global search box (Ctrl+K), the dashboard and the products (Ctrl+1, Ctrl+2, the number pad counting), the sidebar toggle (Ctrl+B), Back (Alt+←, when there is a trail), the product list refresh (F5), the toast dismissal (Esc, when a toast has the keyboard), the reference (F1), and in the products scope the quick inspect (Ctrl+I) and its close (Esc, while open); `Gesture(key, modifiers)` writes a gesture the way a person reads it, `Hint(label, commandKey)` appends it to a tooltip and leaves an action without a shortcut alone, `Find`, `Match(key, modifiers, scope)`, `Filter(query)` through the Turkish-safe fold, `Conflicts()` (two commands sharing a gesture in one scope — there are none), and `Binding(commandKey, command)` builds a control's key binding from the catalogue so a binding and the reference cannot drift apart. Nothing destructive and nothing that writes to a marketplace has a shortcut; Delete is bound nowhere; a shortcut never steps around a confirmation. `MainWindow.UX.cs`: the key handler is `HandleShortcut(key, modifiers)` reading the catalogue — a disabled command (Back with no trail, Escape away from a toast) is not handled, so the key keeps its ordinary meaning; the same method drives the tests. `MainWindow.Filters.cs`: the product grid's and the inspect drawer's bindings come from the catalogue. Hints: Back "Geri (Alt+←)", the global search box "Genel arama (Ctrl+K)", the sidebar toggle through the catalogue, and a new header button "?" named "Klavye kısayolları (F1)" (`MainWindow.xaml`, `MainWindow.xaml.cs`, `MainWindow.Navigation.cs`). `KeyboardShortcutReference.cs` (new): the searchable reference on the dialog shell — a filter box that already has the keyboard on open, the entries with label, gesture and condition, an empty-result line, a note that destructive and live-writing actions have no shortcut and that confirmations cannot be bypassed from the keyboard, and Kapat on Escape.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/KeyboardShortcutsTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): the catalogue is non-empty, labelled, never destructive, one key per command, conflict-free; gestures read as a person reads them and an action without a shortcut gets no hint; the real commands are there, nothing deletes, Ctrl+S is not bound; matching by scope, the number pad, a products shortcut not a shell one; the filter with an empty query, a Turkish word in capitals, a gesture, an ASCII I finding a dotless ı, and a miss. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/KeyboardShortcutsUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window — the hints on Back, the search box, the sidebar toggle and the header button (named), the product grid's bindings equal to the catalogue's products entries, Back not handled with an empty trail and the page kept, Ctrl+2 and the number pad's 1 navigating, Delete handled by nothing, and F1 opening the reference (driven inside its modal loop) with the filter box already focused, "geri" leaving the Back entry, "KISAYOL" finding the reference by its dotless ı, the empty query listing everything, and Escape through the input manager closing it.

## #870 — Context-menu keyboard parity (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-870-row-action-menu off codex/issue-869-shortcut-reference (e52ac8d)

The code had no context menu at all, so parity meant one shared row menu with the keyboard built in. `RowActions.cs` (new): `RowAction(Key, Label, Execute, DisabledReason, Destructive, Gesture)` and `RowActionMenu.Attach(owner, actionsFor, onError)` — the owner gets a menu; the menu key or Shift+F10 on the owner (its own preview key handler, so it does not depend on what the framework does with the key afterwards) and a right-click both open it, rebuilt from the selection at that moment; from the keyboard it sits at the focused row (`FocusTarget`: the grid row or list item the keyboard is in) rather than at the mouse, and the keyboard returns to that row when the menu closes; a disabled action carries its reason as a tooltip that shows while disabled (and in its automation name) and never runs even if clicked; a destructive action's label says a confirmation follows ("…") and its Execute is the same guarded method the button calls, so a menu never bypasses a confirmation; every item is a hit target in DIP. Wired: the product grid (`MainWindow.Filters.cs` `ProductRowActions`: quick inspect with the catalogue's Ctrl+I, activate and deactivate with the count — deactivating many goes through the typed confirmation as before — delete for a single product only through the confirm dialog, copy the SKU or SKUs), the orders grid (`OrdersPanel.cs`: open the detail, copy the order number, copy the tracking number with "Takip numarası yok." as the reason when there is none), and the source list (`MainWindow.xaml.cs` `SourceRowActions`: read the feed, check the source's health — the page's own buttons, with "Önce kaynak seçin." when none is picked). Two framework facts settled on the way: the framework opens a menu on the menu key's key-up and on Shift+F10's key-down, raising the opening event on the focused element with no cursor position, so the owner's key handler opens first and the opening handler leaves an open menu alone; and a key event pushed through the input manager is seen only by its post-process handlers, never routed to elements, so a test raises the preview key-down on the focused element the way physical input arrives.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/RowActionsTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): on a bare grid — the menu key opens the menu anchored at the focused row with bottom placement, the items with their labels, gesture, the destructive ellipsis and the hit-target height, the destructive item asking its guard and doing nothing when refused, the keyboard back on the row after closing, two rows giving the count and a disabled delete with its reason shown while disabled and inert when clicked while the enabled action runs for the selection, no selection giving every action its reason, and the same DIP heights under a 2× scale. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/RowActionsUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window — the product menu with its real actions, the gesture from the catalogue, inspect opening the drawer, two selected giving the count and refusing delete with its reason; the order menu with the detail and the numbers and the tracking reason and nothing destructive; the source menu live with a source picked and every action explaining itself with none picked.

## #871 — Disabled command reason tooltips (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-871-disabled-reasons off codex/issue-870-row-action-menu (bda4732)

One shared way a command is disabled with its reason. `CommandState.cs` (new): `DisabledReasonKind` (Capability, Validation, Selection, StoreState, Busy) and `DisabledReason(Kind, Text)` with a factory per kind, `ForCapability(capabilities, operation, channel)` that yields a reason only for an operation the real `MarketplaceCapabilities` set lacks (nothing invented), and `Describe()` — the kind's word, then the text through the central redaction (a reason never carries a secret); `CommandState.Apply(element, reason, reasonLine?)` sets IsEnabled from the reason, joins the reason to the ordinary tooltip (a disabled command keeps saying what it does, and its shortcut, and adds why it cannot right now — the first full run showed Back losing its "Alt+←" hint) and makes the tooltip show while disabled, remembers the ordinary tooltip the first time a reason joins it and brings it back alone when enabled, writes the automation help text for a screen reader, keeps the structured reason on the element (`ReasonOf`) — and, because a whole editor is disabled at once when nothing is loaded, `ReasonFor(element)` finds the nearest ancestor's reason; `ReasonLine(highContrast)` is a wrapping hint tagged command-reason, hidden until a reason arrives, announced politely, in the system's disabled text colour under high contrast, for the caller who wants the reason in plain view (a keyboard user cannot focus a disabled control). Wired: Back ("Geri gidilecek iz yok." — `MainWindow.Navigation.cs` `RefreshBackButton`, also at start), the product editor as a whole ("Önce ürün seçin." — `MainWindow.xaml.cs`, `MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs`), the XML preview toolbar's apply, recompute and row-diff (the diff distinguishes no preview from the wrong selection) and the import stepper's chips (their stage hint kept in the ordinary tooltip), the report setup's run (busy, or the count of blocking findings), export, columns, saved-filter load and delete, and the store combo (`ReportParameterPanel.cs`), the orders bar's refresh, add and cancel and the stock apply ("Stok bu sipariş için zaten düşüldü." — `OrdersPanel.cs`), the Excel apply, cancel and rejected-rows export (`MainWindow.Excel.cs`), the bulk drawer's preview, approve and apply (`ChannelMatrixBulkDrawer.cs`), the channel product plan's save, previous and next (`ChannelProductsPanel.cs`), the dashboard refresh and the global search (`DashboardPanel.cs`, `MainWindow.UX.cs`). A hidden button (the report's cancel before a run) needs no reason.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/CommandStateTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): each kind applied to a button — disabled, the tooltip the ordinary text joined with the reason and shown while disabled, the help text, the structured reason, the reason line visible with the text; enabled again the ordinary tooltip back, the help text cleared, the line hidden; a control without an ordinary tooltip gets none back; a capability reason only for a missing operation; a bearer value and an address masked out of a reason; the line's brush as a hint and the system's disabled colour under high contrast; a long Turkish reason wrapping beside its button with nothing cut. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/CommandStateUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window — Back's store-state reason with its help text, the XML page's row-diff and apply reasons, the orders cancel reason, the report setup's export reason with its cancel hidden, and a sweep of the dashboard, products, XML, orders and reports pages in which no visible disabled button is left without a reason of its own or of the panel around it (the first sweep found six: the product editor's buttons and the stepper chips).

## #872 — Focus restore after async refresh (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-872-focus-restore off codex/issue-871-disabled-reasons (11939c2)

A refresh rebinds a list and the framework drops the keyboard on the floor. `FocusRestore.cs` (new): `Capture(owner, keyOf)` records where the keyboard is inside a grid or a list — the entity's stable key, the cell's column, the row's index — or that it is elsewhere; `Restore(owner, snapshot, keyOf)` after the rebind puts it back on the same entity's row and cell when the entity is still listed (`Same`), on the row at the same index or the last when the entity is gone (`Neighbour`, deterministic), on the list itself when it is empty (`Owner`), and leaves it alone when it was elsewhere (`Untouched`) — so a person typing in the search box that caused the refresh keeps typing, and a filter combo keeps the keyboard through the rebind it triggers. Selection stays the owners' business (they already restore it by key). Wired: the product grid's `ShowProducts` (every refresh, search and paging path lands there), the orders grid's `Filter()` (search, filters, urgency sort — the same rebind), and the source list's `RefreshSources`. A failed refresh never reaches a rebind, so the keyboard stays where it was.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FocusRestoreTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): on a bare grid — the captured key, column and index; the same cell after a rebind with fresh objects; the neighbour at the same index when the entity is gone; the last row when the list shrank; the list itself when empty; the search box untouched when it had the keyboard; and a list box through the same and the neighbour. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FocusRestoreUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window — the keyboard back on the same product's cell and column after a refresh, on the row that took its place after that product is deleted and the list refreshes, left in the search box across a refresh, on the same order across the urgency-sort rebind, and on the same source across the list's refresh.

Not done: the other grids (sync, media, matrix, channel products, connections, reports) do not restore the keyboard yet; a refresh that fails is untested on the real window (the orders Etsy refresh needs a connection to fail meaningfully; the shared piece is not involved in a failure since no rebind happens); the restore lands on the cell at the same display index, not on the same logical column when columns were reordered in between; the product page's paging keeps the index, not the entity, across pages by design; no announcement is made to a screen reader when the keyboard lands on a neighbour instead of the same entity.

## #873 — Selection persistence across refresh (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-873-selection-persistence off codex/issue-872-focus-restore (73f5853)

The selection is kept by the entities, not by the objects or their positions. `SelectionPersistence.cs` (new): `Capture(owner, keyOf, scope)` records the selected entities' stable keys, the anchor first, and the scope (a store) the selection belongs to; `Restore(owner, snapshot, keyOf, scope)` after a rebind selects again every entity that is listed again (the anchor first, so a single-selection owner and the product card follow it), drops the ones that are gone, and returns how many are kept and how many are missing with their keys; `SelectionRestoreResult.Note` is the plain-words line a list shows — "N seçili öğe bu listede görünmüyor (silinmiş, filtre dışı veya başka sayfada); seçimden çıkarıldı." — and nothing when all of it is back; a snapshot from one scope restores nothing in another, so a selection never crosses a store. Wired: the product grid's `ShowProducts` (every refresh, search, sort, filter and page lands there; the card's product is the fallback when nothing was selected, as before) with the note on the #795 selection bar (`MainWindow.Filters.cs`: the bar stays visible for the note even when nothing is left selected, headline "Seçim güncellendi", and the note clears at the next change of selection by hand), and the bulk panel's `Load` (its search reloads the list) with the note on its status line. The orders grid and the source list already kept a single selection by key with an explicit line for a vanished order.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SelectionPersistenceTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): on a bare grid — the captured keys with the anchor first; a rebind with fresh objects keeping all three and the anchor; a sort keeping all; a delete and a filter keeping one and naming the two gone with the plain-words note; a page listing none keeping nothing; an empty selection restoring nothing; a snapshot from one store restoring nothing in another and everything in its own; and a single-selection list box through the same entity and its loss. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SelectionPersistenceUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window — three products selected surviving a refresh with the card on the anchor and no note, one deleted elsewhere dropped with the bar saying so, a selection by hand clearing the note, and the bulk panel's two selected products surviving the reload its search causes and a narrower search dropping both with the status line saying so.

## #874 — Optimistic UI rollback pattern (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-874-optimistic-rollback off codex/issue-873-selection-persistence (7546b03)

One pattern for the actions the screen shows before the store confirms. `OptimisticMutation.cs` (new): `Run(key, apply, commit, revert, reportError, scope)` and `RunAsync(…, cancellation, scope)` — the screen takes the change at once, the store is asked to keep it, and on any failure (a validation, a database failure, a cancellation) the prior state comes back and a visible, redacted error says so (`Describe`: the cancellation by name, everything else through the central redaction behind "Kaydedilemedi; önceki durum geri alındı."); a second click on the same key while the first is in flight is refused before it applies anything ("İşlem zaten sürüyor; ikinci tıklama yok sayıldı."); a `MutationScope.LiveMarketplace` mutation is refused before anything changes ("Canlı pazaryeri yazımı iyimser uygulanmaz; açık onay ve idempotency korunur."), so the live-write guards of #821 and the idempotent sync intents stay the only road to a marketplace; a revert that fails itself is reported in the same words ("Önceki durum da geri yüklenemedi; ekranı yenileyin.") and never thrown out of a click — the first real-path run showed why: the snooze store's listing purges expired snoozes, a write inside a read, so a store that refuses a write refuses the re-read that a naive revert would do. Wired: the dashboard's notification centre (`DashboardPanel.cs`): acknowledge, un-acknowledge, snooze and un-snooze re-render the list at once with the change applied locally, commit to the notification and snooze stores, and on a refusal redraw from the inputs already in hand (never from a fresh read) and show the error on the dashboard's own error surface with a retry of the same mutation. The product card's and the source form's saves were already pessimistic (the store first, the baseline after), so a failure there leaves the prior state untouched by construction; they were not changed.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OptimisticMutationTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): success committed with nothing reverted; a validation failure reverted with the rule's words; a database failure through the database's own exception; a bearer value masked out of the error; cancellation by a cancelled token and by a commit that observes it; a duplicate click refused while the first is in flight and the first still committing, a different key independent; a live marketplace write refused with nothing applied or committed, synchronously and asynchronously; the key free afterwards; a revert that fails itself reported with both messages and no exception. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OptimisticMutationUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window's dashboard on one of its own alerts (a hand-seeded alert never renders: the dashboard's sync resolves any alert its evaluation does not produce) — acknowledge shows at once and the store keeps it; with the notification database made read-only the un-acknowledge shows at once, the store refuses, the row returns to its stored state and the dashboard's error surface says the prior state was restored, with the refusal's words redacted.

## #875 — View preference schema versioning (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-875-preference-schema off codex/issue-874-optimistic-rollback (2fbde40)

Every persisted UI preference carries its schema version. `PreferenceSchema.cs` (new): an envelope `{"schema":N,"payload":"…"}` around the value the store keeps; `PreferenceFamily(Name, KeyPrefix, CurrentVersion, Migrate)` and a registry of the families the app writes — the product layout, density and hidden columns, the last route, the sidebar, the recent XML source, the dashboard's store filter, the orders preset, custom layout and split, and the report columns (an unregistered key is version 1 with the identity migration, so a new screen is never refused; `Register` adds or replaces a family); `Inspect(store, key)` reads a record — nothing stored (`Missing`), the current version (`Current`), an older version brought forward by the family's migration and re-saved in the current envelope (`Migrated`; a bare value written before the envelope existed is version 0), or the defaults (`Fallback`) for a future version, a corrupt envelope, a migration that cannot bring the record forward, or a store that cannot be read — each fallback recorded in `Diagnostics` as the key and the reason, never the payload; `Read` is the payload alone; `Write` wraps the payload in the family's current envelope and refuses one that the central redaction would change (personal data or a secret never reaches a preference record). Wired at every read and write site: the main window's product layout, density and hidden columns, last route, sidebar and recent source; the dashboard's store filter; the report setup's column layout; the orders workspace's split and its column presets (which took the store's methods as delegates and now take the schema's). The store keys keep their store scope (the report columns per report, the dashboard filter's store key); a layout that names a removed column drops it, as the layout codec already did.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/PreferenceSchemaTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): the envelope round trip, a bare legacy layout and a broken envelope not read as envelopes; missing; a bare legacy density brought forward, re-saved and current next time; a written layout current across a restart of the store; a future version falling back with a diagnostic naming the key and the reason and not the payload; a corrupt envelope falling back; a family whose migration refuses version 0 and brings version 1 forward to 2; an address and a bearer value refused before the write, a GUID id and a column list accepted; the registry covering every key the app writes and a new key not refused; a persisted layout naming a removed column dropping it. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/PreferenceSchemaUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window opened over a bare legacy density, a corrupt sidebar record and a future last route — the default page, the expanded sidebar, the legacy density applied and re-saved in the current envelope, the two fallbacks in the diagnostics without their payloads, the route the window writes an envelope of the current version, and a second window (a restart) reading the migrated density as current and the written route back. Five earlier tests that read the store raw now read through the schema (the envelope is the new contract).

## #876 — Corrupt preference recovery (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-876-preference-recovery off codex/issue-875-preference-schema (e33b36c)

A corrupt UI preference never stops the application from starting, and what fell back is visible and resettable. `PreferenceSchema.cs` (extended, the owner of #875's envelope): `TryDecode<T>(store, key, PreferenceDecoder<T>, out value)` reads a record through the consumer's own decoder and, when the payload is not understood — malformed JSON, a value nobody knows, a route that no longer exists, a decoder that throws — records a diagnostic naming the key and the fixed reason ("içerik anlaşılamadı; varsayılan uygulandı"), never the payload, and the caller keeps its default; nothing stored records nothing; the diagnostics are distinct lines, so a re-read adds nothing. `OpenStore(directory)` is how every production site now obtains the store (window, dashboard, sync centre, orders, XML views, report catalog, reports page): a file that is not a database (or is damaged beyond opening) is moved aside with its journal files as `ui-preferences.corrupt-<utc stamp>.db` and an empty store is created in its place, with a diagnostic that names the file set aside and the store's redacted reason — a second failure is the directory's problem and propagates. `Write` still refuses personal data and secrets, but a store that cannot take the write (read-only, locked) is now a "yazılamadı" diagnostic rather than an exception out of a navigation or a column drag. `Reset(store)` removes every preference record (the store's saved views stay) and clears the diagnostics. The decoders gained their Try forms with the old forms delegating: `DataGridLayoutCodec.TryDeserialize` (a layout without its version is not a layout; a column without a key is dropped, the rest stays), `NavigationSidebar.TryParse` (a JSON object is understood, a missing or unusable field takes its default — partial fields keep what is valid), `ProductListDensity.TryNormalize` (only the two words in either language), `OrderWorkspaceLayout.TryDeserialize` (a finite number in the usable band). Consumers rewired: the last route (a route the shell does not register is a fallback, not a silent default), the sidebar, the product layout and density, the orders split. `UiPreferenceStore.Keys()`/`Clear()`. `DiagnosticsService.Build` adds the check "UI tercihleri" (OK / WARN with the count and the key-and-reason lines, sanitized), which the diagnostics grid shows and the support package's diagnostics.json carries — the package never contained the preference store and still does not. `DiagnosticsPanel`: the action "Görünüm tercihlerini sıfırla" confirms through `DialogShell.Confirm` (destructive: Enter cancels), resets, writes an audit row (module preferences, the count only), refreshes the checks and reports "N tercih kaydı silindi; varsayılanlar bir sonraki açılışta uygulanır."

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/PreferenceRecoveryTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): malformed JSON inside a valid envelope → default + one diagnostic without the payload, unchanged by a second read; an unknown density → diagnostic, "Sık"/"rahat" understood; partial fields (a keyless layout column dropped, a sidebar without a width) kept; a layout without its version, a non-object sidebar, an out-of-band split refused; a future version listed without its payload; nothing stored → no diagnostic; the diagnostics check WARN naming the keys, the support package carrying the check and none of the payload markers nor the store file; a reset removing 3 records, keeping the saved view, clearing the diagnostics, the check OK again; a store file that is not a database → the plain store throws `SqliteException`, `OpenStore` sets the file aside (contents intact), recreates the store, names the file and not its bytes or the directory, a restart is quiet; a read-only store → `Write` records "yazılamadı" and does not throw, the personal-data refusal still throws. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/PreferenceRecoveryUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window over a garbage store file opens and sets the file aside; then, over a malformed layout, an unknown density, a route that does not exist, a non-numeric orders split and a partial sidebar record, opens on the default page with the collapsed sidebar (the partial record's valid field) and the comfortable density, lists the four fallbacks without a payload and not the partial record, shows the "UI tercihleri" WARN row on the diagnostics page, and after the reset's confirmation (the modal driven from inside ShowDialog) has an empty store, the report line and an OK row; a restart opens on the defaults with nothing to report.

Not done: the reset takes effect at the next start (the page says so) — the open window keeps its in-memory layout, density and sidebar until then; the orders column presets, the report column layouts and the dashboard's store filter still decode through their own null-on-untrusted readers without a diagnostic (a saved store key that is gone is a removed store, not corruption); the saved views (`UiViews`, the named sync/order/XML views) are neither enveloped nor covered by the reset or the diagnostics; a store that opens but is damaged on a later page (a read failing mid-session) is the existing "okunamadı" fallback per key, not a quarantine; the quarantined files are kept forever (no pruning); the diagnostics list lives in the process (it is not written to disk), so the support package carries only what fell back since the current start.

## #877 — Window geometry safe restore (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-877-window-geometry off codex/issue-876-preference-recovery (102e31f)

The main window remembers its geometry and restores it only where it can be reached. Before this the shell had no geometry persistence at all (XAML 1440×900, MinWidth 1150, MinHeight 760, CenterScreen every start). `WindowGeometry.cs` (new): `WindowBounds` (DIPs), `WindowGeometryState(Bounds, Maximized)`, `MonitorArea(WorkArea, Primary)`, `WindowRestore(Bounds, Maximized, Outcome)` with `WindowRestoreOutcome` Default / Restored / Fitted. `Fit(saved, monitors, minWidth, minHeight, defaultWidth, defaultHeight)` is the rule: the size is clamped to the monitors' bounding box and never below the window's minimum; a rectangle whose title strip is reachable (its full 32 DIP height inside a work area, at least 160 DIP of its width on monitors — a window straddling two present monitors included) keeps its position and only what hangs beyond the bounding box is pulled in, unless that pull would put the strip into a gap between monitors; anything else — a monitor that was removed, a title bar above or beside every screen, a title bar in the gap above a lower second monitor — is moved onto the monitor it overlaps most (the primary when none) and clamped inside that work area, top-left aligned when bigger than the area, so a DPI change that leaves a smaller work area in DIPs shrinks the window to fit with the title bar still at hand; nothing saved (or no monitor known) is Default, centred on the primary. `Capture(state, normalBounds)` never remembers a minimized state. `Serialize`/`TryParse` (a JSON object with finite Left/Top/Width/Height within sane limits, Maximized optional; anything else refused, so through `PreferenceSchema.TryDecode` a corrupt record is a "shell:window" diagnostic naming the key and the start-up placement stands). `CurrentMonitors()` asks the system (user32 EnumDisplayMonitors + GetMonitorInfoW, physical work areas divided by GetDpiForSystem — the application is system-DPI-aware) and falls back to the primary work area. `Restore(window, store)` runs in the constructor right after the store is opened: Default leaves the XAML placement, otherwise Manual placement with the fitted rectangle and the maximized state; `Save(window, store)` runs in `OnClosing` once the close is certain (after the unsaved-draft prompt), writing the restore bounds (the normal rectangle while maximized or minimized) and the maximized flag, nothing when the bounds are unusable — a window that was never shown has a NaN position, and the scheduler and OAuth fixtures close such windows (the first full run caught a JSON NaN failure there). `PreferenceSchema` registers the family "window" for `shell:window`.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/WindowGeometryTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): restored untouched on a present monitor and when straddling two; removed second monitor → back onto the primary at the same size, as far right as it fits; DPI change (1280×680 work area) → 1280×760 at the top-left (never below the minimum); a title bar above the screen pulled down; a window mostly beyond the left edge pulled in; one hanging off the right edge pulled in; one whose title bar sits in the gap above a lower second monitor pulled down onto it although it lay inside the bounding box; maximized comes back maximized on a monitor that exists; nothing saved or no monitor → Default centred (240, 70); a round trip, the optional flag, nine corrupt records refused (NaN as a string, a negative width, a missing height, an absurd coordinate, an absurd extent, an array, truncated JSON, empty, a word); a minimized capture is not maximized; through the schema a corrupt record is a diagnostic without the payload and the family is registered. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/WindowGeometryUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window over a geometry saved 3000 DIP beyond the right-most monitor of this machine opens exactly where `Fit` puts it on the real monitors (Manual placement, Left/Top/ActualWidth/ActualHeight within 1 DIP, not off-screen); a close while minimized saves the normal bounds and no minimized state; a corrupt record leaves CenterScreen and a Normal window and is named in the diagnostics; a maximized close keeps the flag and a restart comes back maximized.

Not done: the geometry is applied once at start-up — a monitor unplugged while the application runs is Windows' own concern (the OS moves windows off a removed display); a per-monitor DPI change at run time is not re-fitted (the application is system-DPI-aware, so all DIPs scale together); the placement is not re-fitted when the window is restored from maximized by the user; the dialogs (`DialogShell`) keep their own centring and are not remembered; the saved rectangle is not tied to a monitor identity, so a rectangle that happens to fit a differently arranged set of monitors is restored there; no diagnostic is recorded for a fitted (moved or resized) restore, only for a corrupt record.

## #878 — UI render latency instrumentation (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-878-ui-latency off codex/issue-877-window-geometry (90b0c56)

Every product, order, dashboard and import screen load or refresh is measured at its boundary and recorded as a structured, PII-safe metric. `UiLatency.cs` (new): `LatencyPhase` Load / Refresh, `LatencyOutcome` Completed / Cancelled / Failed / Superseded, `LatencyMetric(AtUtc, View, Phase, Outcome, DurationMs, Count, Scope, Warm, Correlation)`, `LatencySummary(View, Phase, Samples, MedianMs, P95Ms, MaxMs, Failed, Cancelled)`. `UiLatency.Begin(store, view, phase, scope)` returns a `LatencyScope` with its own clock and 12-character correlation id whose first verdict — `Complete(count)`, `Cancel()`, `Fail()`, `Supersede()` — records the row and wins over any later one; a view name that is not a fixed lowercase identifier is a programming error (`ArgumentException`), a scope that is not an id shape (a store key, a source id: letters, digits, `:_.-`, at most 64) is recorded as `invalid-scope` and never as its text, an empty scope is `all`, a negative count is 0; a store that cannot take the row never throws into the screen. Cold/warm: `LatencyStore.MarkSeen`/`HasSeen`/`PhaseFor` — the first verdict of a view in the session is cold and its load, the next ones warm and refreshes. `LatencyStore` (`ui-latency.db`, table `UiLatency`, retention 5000 rows pruned on every insert, `List(limit, view)` newest first, `Summary()` per view and phase with nearest-rank median and p95 over completed and superseded durations plus failure and cancellation counts, `Percentile`). `UiLatency.Check(summaries)` is the diagnostics line "Ekran gecikmesi": OK, WARN when any p95 crosses 2000 ms, "Henüz ölçüm yok" before the first measurement; `DiagnosticsService.Build` adds it and the support package gains `ui-latency.json` (the summary and the 200 most recent rows — ids, durations, counts, outcomes). Boundaries wired: the main window's `RefreshProducts` (synchronous: Load the first time, Refresh after, count = the page's items) and `SearchProductsAsync` (a stale revision is Superseded, an exception Failed), `InspectAsync` (import Load scoped by the source id; the download and read failures Fail, the operator's cancel Cancel; count = the field paths found) and `PreviewAsync` (import Refresh; parse/validate/preview failures Fail, cancel Cancel; count = the preview rows); `DashboardPanel.RefreshAsync` (Load for the page's own first read, Refresh for the "Durumu yenile" click, scoped by the store filter key, count = connections; the catch Fails); `OrdersPanel.Load` (a wrapper around the former body, now `LoadCore`; count = the orders read). Each owner constructs its own `LatencyStore` on the data directory.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/UiLatencyTests.cs` (new, 1, red first): cold then warm with the boundary's own clock and count; another view cold on its own first load; cancelled, failed and superseded outcomes; the first verdict wins and one row per scope; two measurements in flight keeping separate clocks and correlations; a store key passes, an e-mail, a product-like name and an empty scope are `invalid-scope`/`all`, a non-identifier view is refused, a negative count is 0; rows newest first and none carrying `@` or the marker; a summary over durations 10..50 → median 30, p95 50, max 50, one failure and one cancellation counted, an empty percentile 0; a retention of 20 keeping the newest 20 of 25; the diagnostics check OK with the views and "p95", a 2001 ms row turning it WARN, the support package carrying `ui-latency.json` with the views and none of the markers; a read-only store still returning the verdict without an exception. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/UiLatencyUiTests.cs` (new, 1): the real main window over two seeded products named "Kırmızı Elbise" and a feed source titled with a marker — the product list's construction-time load is a cold "products" load with count 2, a search "SKU-1" a warm refresh with count 1, the orders page records its load, the dashboard its first load and the "Durumu yenile" click as a warm refresh, reading the XML source an "import" load scoped "feed-1" with the field count; no row's view, scope or correlation carries the product name, the marker or a space, and the diagnostics line names the import view without the product name.
